to be a copy of the same hypercone, possibly rotated.

Proof.

Consider any
p_{i}\in\operatorname{sing}\mathscr{F}
,
\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}_{i}}(\Sigma_{i};p_{i},2^{-\ell_{i}})<\varepsilon_{i}\to 0
and
\ell_{i}\to\infty
as in the setup.

Choose any linear isometry
\iota_{p_{i}}:\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\to(T_{p_{i}}M,g_{p_{i}})
. Passing to a subsequence, and using
\ell_{i}\to\infty
, we may assume that

(\eta_{p_{i},2^{-\ell_{i}}}\circ\iota_{p_{i}})^{-1}\;\Sigma_{i}\to\Sigma,

a minimizer in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, and

\iota_{p_{i}}^{-1}\;\mathcal{C}_{i}\to\mathcal{C},

a nonflat minimizing hypercone in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. Also,
\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}_{i}}(\Sigma_{i};p_{i},2^{-\ell_{i}})<\varepsilon_{i}\to 0
implies that

(7.1)

(\eta_{p_{i},2^{-\ell_{i}}}\circ\iota_{p_{i}})^{-1}\;\Sigma_{i}\to\mathcal{C}.

Let us rule out (i), (ii), (iii), or (iv) failing as
i\to\infty
with
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i}:=\mathcal{C}
. By passing to a further subsequence, we may assume that one of (i), (ii), (iii), (iv) fails consistently as
i\to\infty
. We immediately observe that (iv) could not have failed as
i\to\infty
with
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i}=\mathcal{C}
since
\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
is discrete modulo rotations (Corollary
A.5
). So we focus on (i), (ii), (iii).

To set things up, consider any
p_{i}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{sing}\mathscr{F}
with
\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime};p_{i}^{\prime},2^{-\ell_{i}})<\varepsilon_{i}
for its corresponding
\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}
. Then,

(7.2)

(\eta_{p_{i},2^{-\ell_{i}}}\circ\iota_{p_{i}})^{-1}p_{i}^{\prime}=:\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\prime}\in B_{1}.

Passing to a subsequence again, we may arrange for

(7.3)

\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in\bar{B}_{1}.

and

(\eta_{p_{i},2^{-\ell_{i}}}\circ\iota_{p_{i}})^{-1}\;\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}\to\Sigma^{\prime},

a minimizer, and

(\iota_{p_{i}}\circ\iota_{p_{i}\to p_{i}^{\prime}})^{-1}\;\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime},

a nonflat minimizing hypercone in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. It follows from
\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime};p_{i}^{\prime},2^{-\ell_{i}})<\varepsilon_{i}\to 0
that
\Sigma^{\prime}-\mathbf{v}^{\prime}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, i.e.,

(7.4)

(\eta_{p_{i},2^{-\ell_{i}}}\circ\iota_{p_{i}})^{-1}\;\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}+\mathbf{v}^{\prime}.

Then, (
7.1
), (
7.2
), (
7.3
), (
7.4
), the fact that elements of
\mathscr{F}
do not cross smoothly, and Lemma
B.1
guarantee that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=\mathcal{C}
after perhaps swapping orientations, and

(7.5)

\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}.

Let us first see why (i) cannot fail as
i\to\infty
with
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i}:=\mathcal{C}
. If it did, then (
7.2
) turns into

\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\prime}\in B_{1}\setminus B_{\theta}(V),

with
V=\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}
, and taking limits yields

\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in\bar{B}_{1}\setminus B_{\theta}(V).

Clearly, this contradicts (
7.5
), so (i) could not have failed as
i\to\infty
.

Suppose now that (ii) fails as
i\to\infty
.
The argument above with
(p_{i}^{+},\Sigma_{i}^{+})
in place of
(p_{i}^{\prime},\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})
shows that

(\eta_{p_{i},2^{-\ell_{i}}}\circ\iota_{p_{i}})^{-1}\;\operatorname{reg}_{>\theta\sigma}\Sigma^{+}_{i}

converge locally to a superset of
\operatorname{reg}_{>2\theta\sigma}\mathcal{C}
, and the result follows by Lemma
C.1
(i), applied with
\mathcal{C}=\theta(\mathcal{C}+\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
in view of (
7.1
), (
7.2
), (
7.3
), (
7.4
), (
7.5
). Thus, (ii) could also not have failed as
i\to\infty
.

Finally suppose that (iii) fails with
p_{i}^{\prime}
,
p_{i}^{\pm}
,
\Sigma_{i}^{\pm}
and
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i}:=\mathcal{C}
. The argument above with
(p_{i}^{\pm},\Sigma_{i}^{\pm})
in place of
(p_{i}^{\prime},\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})
implies

(\eta_{p_{i},2^{-\ell_{i}}}\circ\iota_{p_{i}})^{-1}\;\Sigma^{\pm}_{i}\to\mathcal{C}.

We can take the difference of the corresponding graphical functions over an exhausting sequence of subsets of
\mathcal{C}
and use the standard Harnack inequality and the connectedness of
\mathcal{C}
to define a positive Jacobi field
u
on
\mathcal{C}
. Then the failure of the distance estimate rescales to give, in the limit as
i\to\infty
, a left-hand side that is

\leq\frac{\inf_{\operatorname{reg}_{>2\sigma}\mathcal{C}\cap\partial B_{1}(\mathbf{0})}u}{1},

and a right-hand side that is, by (
7.2
) and (
7.3
),

\geq H\cdot\theta^{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma/2)}\cdot\frac{\inf_{\operatorname{reg}_{>\frac{1}{2}\theta\sigma}\mathcal{C}\cap\partial B_{\theta}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})}u}{\theta}.

This contradicts Lemma
C.1
(ii) and thus completes the proof.
∎

7.3.
Singular set stratification via density drop

We will use the following stratification of
\operatorname{sing}\mathscr{F}
that exploits monotonicity:

Definition 7.2
.

For
\eta>0
,
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, define

\mathcal{S}(\eta,L):=\cup_{\Sigma\in\mathscr{F}}\{p\in\operatorname{sing}\Sigma:\Theta^{1}_{\Sigma}(p,2^{-L})-\Theta_{\Sigma}(p)\leq\eta\}.

Remark 7.3
.

The monotonicity property of
\Theta^{1}_{\Sigma}(p,\cdot)
from Section
4
implies:

(1)

\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})\subset\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{1})
whenever
L_{1}\geq L_{0}
, and

(2)

For each
\eta>0
,
L\mapsto\mathcal{S}(\eta,L)
is an exhaustion of
\operatorname{sing}\mathscr{F}
.

The advantage of this definition is that it combines well with Proposition
7.1
and allows us to invoke it in arbitrarily deep scales, and in particular iterate Proposition
7.1
(iii) to obtain our coarse Hölder estimates. This is done in the two subsequent lemmas.

Lemma 7.4
.

Fix
\sigma,\theta,\varepsilon
as in Proposition
7.1
. There exist
\eta>0
,
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
depending on
n,\sigma,\theta,\varepsilon
with the following significance.

Let
L_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
L_{0}\geq L
. Then, Proposition
7.1
applies with
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})
and any
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
\ell\geq L_{0}
; i.e., for every
p\in\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})
with corresponding
\Sigma\in\mathscr{F}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
\ell\geq L_{0}
, we have

\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}_{p,\ell}}(\Sigma;p,2^{-\ell})<\varepsilon

to some nonflat minimizing hypercone
\mathcal{C}_{p,\ell}\subset T_{p}M
.

Proof.

It is a straightforward argument by contradiction.
∎

To establish the coarse Hölder continuity necessary for Proposition
6.1
, we will iterate Proposition
7.1
along sequences of shrinking balls, specifically, with radii shrinking like
2^{-\ell}\theta^{i}
,
\ell
large but fixed, and
i=0,1,2,\ldots

Lemma 7.5

(Coarse Hölder continuity via iteration)
.

Fix
\sigma,\theta,\varepsilon
as in Proposition
7.1
, with
\theta=2^{-m}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and
\eta,L
as in Lemma
7.4
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
\ell\geq L
. Assume that
p^{\pm},p_{0},\ldots,p_{j}\in\mathcal{S}(\eta,\ell)
satisfy:

(1)

p_{i+1}\in B_{2^{-\ell}\theta^{i}}(p_{i})
for all
i=0,1,\ldots,j-1
, and

(2)

p^{\pm}\in B_{2^{-\ell}\theta^{i}}(p_{i})
for all
i=0,1,\ldots,j
.

Consider
p^{\pm}\in\operatorname{sing}\Sigma^{\pm}
with
\Sigma^{\pm}\in\mathscr{F}
. Then:

(i)

We have the scale invariant distance estimate

d_{j}:=\frac{d_{g}(\operatorname{spt}\Sigma^{-},\operatorname{reg}_{>2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}\sigma}\Sigma^{+}\cap\partial B_{2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}}(p_{j}))}{2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}}\leq 2.

(ii)

We have
22
22

22

We have not directly used (i) to estimate
d_{j}\leq 2
in (ii), as we will want to use a stronger estimate on
d_{j}
when
p^{\pm}
are both modeled by cylinders over quadratic hypercones in
B_{2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}}(p_{j})
.

|\mathfrak{T}(p^{+})-\mathfrak{T}(p^{-})|\leq(\operatorname{Lip}\mathfrak{T})\cdot 2^{-\ell}\cdot d_{j}\cdot H^{j}\cdot\theta^{\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}(1+\alpha(\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i};\sigma))},

where
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\subset T_{p_{i}}M
is the hypercone at
p_{i}\in\mathcal{S}(\eta,\ell)
, scale
2^{-\ell}\theta^{i}
, given by Lemma
7.4
and then Proposition
7.1
, and
H=H(n,\sigma)
is as in Proposition
7.1
.

Proof.

(i) Apply Proposition
7.1
(ii) with

p\mapsto p_{j-1},\;p^{\prime}\mapsto p_{j},\;\text{our given }p^{\pm},\;\text{and }\ell\mapsto\ell+\log_{2}(1/\theta)^{j-1}.

This is possible by Lemma
7.4
, (1) and (2) with
i\mapsto j-1
, and Remark
7.3
. This yields a point

p^{+}_{j}\in\operatorname{reg}_{>2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}\sigma}\Sigma^{+}\cap\partial B_{2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}}(p_{j}).

Then,

d_{j}\leq\frac{d_{g}(p^{-},p^{+}_{j})}{2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}}\leq\frac{d_{g}(p^{-},p_{j})+d_{g}(p_{j},p^{+}_{j})}{2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}}\leq 2

where we used
p^{+}_{j}\in\partial B_{2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}}(p_{j})
,
p^{-}\in B_{2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}}(p_{j})
by (2) with
i\mapsto j
, and the triangle inequality.

(ii) An iteration of Proposition
7.1
(iii) outward from smaller balls to larger balls, i.e., iteratively with
i^{\prime}=1,\ldots,j
and

p\mapsto p_{j-i^{\prime}},\;p^{\prime}\mapsto p_{j-i^{\prime}+1},\;\ell\mapsto\ell+\log_{2}(1/\theta)^{j-i^{\prime}}=\ell+(j-i^{\prime})m,

which is possible by (1) and (2) with
i\mapsto j-i^{\prime}
and Remark
7.3
, yields:

\displaystyle\frac{d_{g}(\operatorname{spt}\Sigma^{-},\operatorname{reg}_{>2^{-\ell}\sigma}\Sigma^{+}\cap\partial B_{2^{-\ell}}(p_{0}))}{2^{-\ell}}

\displaystyle\leq d_{j}\cdot H^{j}\cdot\theta^{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{j}(1+\alpha(\check{\mathcal{C}}_{j-i^{\prime}};\sigma))}

\displaystyle=d_{j}\cdot H^{j}\cdot\theta^{\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}(1+\alpha(\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i};\sigma))}.

Choose
\hat{p}^{\pm}\in\operatorname{spt}\Sigma^{\pm}
attaining
d_{g}
in the left hand side above. Rearranging,

d_{g}(\hat{p}^{+},\hat{p}^{-})\leq 2^{-\ell}\cdot d_{j}\cdot H^{j}\cdot\theta^{\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}(1+\alpha(\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i};\sigma))}.

Using in this order the facts that
\mathfrak{T}
is constant on each
\operatorname{spt}\Sigma^{\pm}
, as well as Lipschitz, and then the bound on
d_{g}(\hat{p}^{+},\hat{p}^{-})
, we get the chain of inequalities:

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{T}(p^{+})-\mathfrak{T}(p^{-})|

\displaystyle=|\mathfrak{T}(\hat{p}^{+})-\mathfrak{T}(\hat{p}^{-})|

\displaystyle\leq(\operatorname{Lip}\mathfrak{T})\cdot d_{g}(\hat{p}^{+},\hat{p}^{-})

\displaystyle\leq(\operatorname{Lip}\mathfrak{T})\cdot 2^{-\ell}\cdot d_{j}\cdot H^{j}\cdot\theta^{\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}(1+\alpha(\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i};\sigma))}.\

This completes the proof.
∎

7.4.
Special case: singular points modeled by the same hypercone

When we invoke Lemma
7.5
on
p^{\pm}
modeled by a copy of the same nonflat minimizing hypercone
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, we can replace the estimate
d_{j}\leq 2
from Lemma
7.5
(i) to one where
d_{j}
is controlled by how close each
\Sigma^{\pm}
is to its corresponding copy
\mathcal{C}^{\pm}\subset T_{p^{\pm}}
of
\mathcal{C}
at scale
2^{-\ell}\theta^{j}
. This can improve the estimate on
|\mathfrak{T}(p^{+})-\mathfrak{T}(p^{-})|
in Lemma
7.5
(ii).

Throughout this section we fix some nonflat minimizing hypercone
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
.

Proposition 7.6
.

There exists
\sigma=\sigma(\mathcal{C})\in(0,\tfrac{1}{8})
with the following property.

If
\mathcal{C}^{\pm}\in\operatorname{Rot}(\mathcal{C})
and
\mathbf{x}^{\pm}\in B_{4\sigma}
, then

(\operatorname{reg}_{>3\sigma}\mathcal{C}^{-}+\mathbf{x}^{-})\cap(\operatorname{reg}_{>3\sigma}\mathcal{C}^{+}+\mathbf{x}^{+})\cap(B_{1}\setminus B_{2\sigma})\not=\emptyset.

Proof.

Rotating and translating a sequence of counterexamples, there is
\mathcal{C}_{j}\in\operatorname{Rot}(\mathcal{C})
and
|\mathbf{x}_{j}|<8j^{-1}
so that

(7.6)

(\operatorname{reg}_{>3j^{-1}}\mathcal{C})\cap(\operatorname{reg}_{>3j^{-1}}\mathcal{C}_{j}+\mathbf{x}_{j})\cap(B_{1/2}\setminus B_{6j^{-1}})=\emptyset.

Letting
j\to\infty
, the strong maximum principle gives that
\mathcal{C}_{j}
limits to
\mathcal{C}
. In particular,

\mathcal{C}_{j}=e^{\mathbf{A}_{j}}\mathcal{C}

for some
\mathbf{A}_{j}\in\mathfrak{so}(n+1)
with
\mathbf{A}_{j}\to\mathbf{O}
as
j\to\infty
. Let’s then rewrite (
7.6
) as:

(7.7)

(\operatorname{reg}_{>3j^{-1}}\mathcal{C})\cap(e^{\mathbf{A}_{j}}\operatorname{reg}_{>3j^{-1}}\mathcal{C}+\mathbf{x}_{j})\cap(B_{1/2}\setminus B_{6j^{-1}})=\emptyset.

(In the middle term, the translation by
\mathbf{x}_{j}
happens after the rotation by
e^{\mathbf{A}_{j}}
.) By suitably modding out by symmetries, we arrange the following:

Claim 7.7
.

There exist
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
,
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{j}\in\mathfrak{so}(n+1)
such that:

(7.8)

(\operatorname{reg}_{>3j^{-1}}\mathcal{C})\cap(e^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{j}}\operatorname{reg}_{>3j^{-1}}\mathcal{C}+\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{j})\cap(B_{1/4}\setminus B_{15j^{-1}})=\emptyset,

(7.9)

0<|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{j}|+|\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{j}|=:\lambda_{j}\to 0,

Proof of claim.

We decompose

\mathbf{x}_{j}=:\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\parallel}+\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\perp}\in\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}\oplus(\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C})^{\perp}.

Moreover, denote

\mathfrak{s}:=\{\mathbf{B}\in\mathfrak{so}(n+1):\langle\mathbf{B}p,\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle=0\text{ for all }p\in\mathcal{C}\},

and let
\mathfrak{s}^{\perp}
be any complementary subspace in
\mathfrak{so}(n+1)
, e.g., one obtained by choosing an inner product and taking orthogonal complements. Then, decompose

\mathbf{A}_{j}=:\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\parallel}+\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\perp}\in\mathfrak{s}\oplus\mathfrak{s}^{\perp}.

Choose

\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\prime}:=e^{-\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\parallel}}\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\perp},

\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\prime}:=\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\perp}+\mathbf{B}_{j},

where
\mathbf{B}_{j}
is chosen so that

e^{\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\perp}+\mathbf{B}_{j}}=e^{-\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\parallel}}e^{\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\parallel}+\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\perp}}(=e^{-\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\parallel}}e^{\mathbf{A}_{j}}).

Note that this can be arranged by Lemma
F.1
, which additionally guarantees that

(7.10)

|\mathbf{B}_{j}|\leq C|\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\parallel}||\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\perp}|.

We may now obtain (
7.8
) from (
7.7
): translate by
-\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\parallel}
, rotate by
e^{-\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\parallel}}
(these actions preserve the
\operatorname{reg}_{>3j^{-1}}\mathcal{C}
on the left), and use
|\mathbf{x}_{j}|<8j^{-1}
.

We fix these
\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\prime}
for the remainder of the proof, and set
\lambda_{j}:=|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\prime}|+|\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\prime}|
. Then, the decay assertion in (
7.9
) immediately follows from
|\mathbf{x}_{j}|\to 0
,
|\mathbf{A}_{j}|\to 0
, and (
7.10
). To see
\lambda_{j}>0
, note that if
\lambda_{j}=0
, then
\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathbf{0}
and
\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathbf{O}
, so (
7.8
) cannot hold. This completes the proof of (
7.9
), and thus the claim.
∎

We also have:

Claim 7.8
.

Assume the notation of Claim
7.7
. Writing

e^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{j}}\mathcal{C}+\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{j}\supset\operatorname{graph}_{\mathcal{C}}h_{j},

for some
h_{j}
over a connected exhaustion of
\mathcal{C}\cap B_{1/4}
, then subsequentially

(7.11)

\lambda_{j}^{-1}h_{j}\to u\in\operatorname{Jac}_{\textnormal{trl}}(\mathcal{C})\oplus\operatorname{Jac}_{\textnormal{rot}}(\mathcal{C})\text{ in }C^{\infty}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathcal{C}\cap B_{1/4}),

and after perhaps swapping unit normals:

(7.12)

u>0.

Proof of claim.

Passing to a subsequence, denote

(7.13)

\lambda_{i}^{-1}(\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\prime},\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\prime})\to(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{B})\in((\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C})^{\perp}\oplus\mathfrak{s}^{\perp})\setminus\{(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{O})\}.

The fact that
\mathbf{B}\in\mathfrak{s}^{\perp}
follows from
\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\perp}\in\mathfrak{s}^{\perp}
and (
7.10
).

We relate
h_{j}
to
\mathbf{y}
,
\mathbf{B}
. Fixing some
p\in\mathcal{C}
(to be determined), we can write
\mathcal{C}
locally as a graph over
T_{p}\mathcal{C}
of some function
h
with
h(\mathbf{0})=|\nabla h(\mathbf{0})|=0
and
|D^{2}h|\leq Cr_{\mathcal{C}}(p)^{-1}
on
\mathcal{C}\cap B_{cr_{\mathcal{C}}(p)}\subset T_{p}\mathcal{C}
. In particular
\|\nabla h\|_{C^{0}(\mathcal{C}\cap B_{t})}\leq Cr_{\mathcal{C}}(p)^{-1}|t|
for
t\in(0,cr_{\mathcal{C}}(p))
. For
j
sufficiently large (depending on
p
), Lemma
G.1
thus applies with
p
,
\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\prime}
in place of
\mathbf{z}
,
\mathbf{x}
,
\mathbf{A}
, to show that

(7.14)

|h_{j}(p)-\langle\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\prime}p+\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\prime},\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle|\leq C(p)(|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\prime}|+|\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\prime}|)^{2}=C(p)\lambda_{j}^{2}

Moreover, by (
7.13
),

(7.15)

|\lambda_{j}^{-1}\langle\mathbf{A}_{j}^{\prime}p+\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\prime},\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle-\langle\mathbf{B}p+\mathbf{y},\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle|\leq\epsilon_{p,i}\to 0

as
i\to\infty
. It follows from (
7.14
), (
7.15
), that

(7.16)

\lambda_{j}^{-1}h_{j}(p)\to\langle\mathbf{B}p+\mathbf{y},\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle\text{ pointwise for }p\in\mathcal{C}\cap B_{1/4}.

Note that the right hand side above is an element of
\operatorname{Jac}_{\textnormal{trl}}(\mathcal{C})\oplus\operatorname{Jac}_{\textnormal{rot}}(\mathcal{C})
(see Definitions
3.2
,
3.3
). This implies (
7.11
).

Moreover, (
7.7
) and (
7.8
) imply that
h_{j}\geq 0
, perhaps after swapping unit normals. Thus, (
7.12
) will follow from (
7.16
), the Harnack inequality, and standard elliptic theory if we can show that the right hand side of (
7.16
) is not identically
0
.

Suppose not. Then,

p\mapsto\langle\mathbf{B}p+\mathbf{y},\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle

is a linear combination of a translation and a rotation Jacobi field that vanishes on
\mathcal{C}\cap B_{1/4}
, and thus on
\mathcal{C}
by unique continuation. Thus, both the translation and the rotation parts vanish:

p\mapsto\langle\mathbf{B}p,\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle\equiv\langle\mathbf{y},\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle\equiv 0,

since one grows linearly and the other does not. But recall that
\langle\mathbf{y},\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle\equiv 0
implies
\mathbf{y}\in\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}
, and
\langle\mathbf{B}p,\nu_{\mathcal{C}}(p)\rangle\equiv 0
implies
\mathbf{B}\in\mathfrak{s}
. This is a contradiction to (
7.13
). This completes the proof that there exists
p\in\mathcal{C}\cap B_{1/4}
so that the right hand side of (
7.16
) is nonvanishing. This completes the proof of (
7.12
) and thus the claim.
∎

The
u
constructed in Claim
7.8
leads to a contradiction. Indeed,
u
is bounded near
\mathbf{0}
, being the sum of translation and a rotation Jacobi fields by (
7.11
), and is positive by (
7.12
). This violates a well-known result of L. Simon
[
Sim08
]
(cf.
[
Wan24
, Theorem 1.4]
) about positive Jacobi fields. This contradiction completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.9
.

There exists
\sigma_{0}=\sigma_{0}(\mathcal{C})>0
such that for all
\sigma\in(0,\sigma_{0})
we can find
\tau_{0}>0
,
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, and
C_{0}>0
depending on
\mathcal{C},\sigma
and have the following properties.

Consider
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
,
p\in\breve{M}
, minimizing
\Sigma^{\pm}
,
p^{\pm}\in B_{\sigma\tau}(p)\cap\operatorname{spt}\Sigma^{\pm}
, and copies
\mathcal{C}^{\pm}=\iota_{p^{\pm}}(\mathcal{C})\subset T_{p^{\pm}}M
such that:

(1)

\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}^{\pm}}(\Sigma^{\pm};p^{\pm},\tau)<\varepsilon_{0}
, and

(2)

\Sigma^{\pm}
do not cross smoothly.

Then, we have the scale-invariant distance estimate:

\frac{d_{g}(\operatorname{spt}\Sigma^{-},\operatorname{reg}_{>\tau\sigma}\Sigma^{+}\cap\partial B_{\tau}(p))}{\tau}\leq C_{0}\cdot(\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}^{+}}(\Sigma^{+};p^{+},\tau)+\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}^{-}}(\Sigma^{-};p^{-},\tau)+\tau^{2}).

Proof.

We always assume
\sigma,\tau<\tfrac{1}{2}
below.

Let
\iota_{p^{\pm}\to p}:(T_{p^{\pm}}M,g_{p^{\pm}})\to(T_{p}M,g_{p})
be the parallel transport map along the minimizing geodesic from
p^{\pm}
to
p
. This is well-defined since
p\in\breve{M}
. Then

\tilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\pm}:=\iota_{p^{\pm}\to p}(\mathcal{C}^{\pm})\subset T_{p}M,

are copies of
\mathcal{C}
in
T_{p}M
. Recalling (
E.2
) and (
E.3
) we can write

p^{\pm}:=\eta_{p,\frac{\tau}{2}}(\mathbf{v}^{\pm}),\;\mathbf{v}^{\pm}\in B_{2\sigma}\subset T_{p}M.

Invoke Proposition
7.6
to find
\sigma:=\sigma(\mathcal{C})<\frac{1}{8}
and
\mathbf{z}\in T_{p}M
so that

\mathbf{z}\in(\operatorname{reg}_{>3\sigma}\tilde{\mathcal{C}}^{-}+\mathbf{v}^{-})\cap(\operatorname{reg}_{>3\sigma}\tilde{\mathcal{C}}^{+}+\mathbf{v}^{+})\cap(B_{1}\setminus B_{2\sigma})\subset T_{p}M.

Let
z=\eta_{p,\frac{\tau}{2}}(\mathbf{z})\in B_{\frac{\tau}{2}}(p)\setminus B_{\sigma\tau}(p)
. Taking
\tau_{0}
small, Lemma
E.2
gives

|\eta_{p^{\pm},\frac{\tau}{2}}^{-1}(z)-\iota_{p\to p^{\pm}}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{v}^{\pm})|\leq C\tau^{2}

where
\iota_{p\to p^{\pm}}=(\iota_{p^{\pm}\to p})^{-1}
is the parallel transport map. As such, we can find
\mathbf{w}^{\pm}\in\operatorname{reg}_{>3\sigma}\mathcal{C}^{\pm}
with

d_{g_{p^{\pm},\frac{\tau}{2}}}(\eta_{p^{\pm},\frac{\tau}{2}}^{-1}(z),\mathbf{w}^{\pm})\leq C\tau^{2}.

Taking
\tau_{0}
even smaller, we have

d_{g}(p^{\pm},\eta_{p^{\pm},\frac{\tau}{2}}(\mathbf{w}^{\pm}))\leq d_{g}(p^{\pm},p)+d_{g}(p,z)+d_{g}(z,\eta_{p^{\pm},\frac{\tau}{2}}(\mathbf{w}^{\pm}))\leq\sigma\tau+\frac{1}{2}\tau+C\tau^{3}\leq\frac{3\tau}{4}.

As such, if we take
\varepsilon_{0},\tau_{0}
even smaller, elliptic estimates (for the graphical function of
(\Sigma^{\pm})_{p^{\pm},\frac{\tau}{2}}
over
\mathcal{C}^{\pm}
near
\mathbf{w}^{\pm}
) and the triangle inequality give

d_{g_{p^{\pm},\frac{\tau}{2}}}(\eta_{p^{\pm},\frac{\tau}{2}}^{-1}(z),\operatorname{reg}_{>2\sigma}(\Sigma^{\pm})_{p^{\pm},\frac{\tau}{2}})\leq C(\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}^{\pm}}(\Sigma^{\pm};p^{\pm},\tau)+\tau^{2}).

Thus

\frac{d_{g}(z,\operatorname{reg}_{>\tau\sigma}\Sigma^{\pm})}{\tau}\leq C(\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}^{\pm}}(\Sigma^{\pm};p^{\pm},\tau)+\tau^{2}).

Taking
\varepsilon_{0},\tau_{0}
even smaller,
z\in B_{\tau}(p)\setminus B_{\sigma\tau}(p)
implies that

\frac{d_{g}(z,\operatorname{reg}_{>\tau\sigma}\Sigma^{\pm}\cap(B_{2\tau}(p)\setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}\sigma\tau}(p)))}{\tau}\leq C(\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}^{\pm}}(\Sigma^{\pm};p^{\pm},\tau)+\tau^{2}).

Using the triangle inequality we thus conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{d_{g}(\operatorname{reg}_{>\tau\sigma}\Sigma^{-},\operatorname{reg}_{>\tau\sigma}\Sigma^{+}\cap(B_{2\tau}(p)\setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}\sigma\tau}(p)))}{\tau}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C(\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}^{+}}(\Sigma^{+};p^{+},\tau)+\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}^{-}}(\Sigma^{-};p^{-},\tau)+\tau^{2}).

Taking
\varepsilon_{0}
,
\tau_{0}
smaller if necessary, the assertion follows from the Harnack inequality as encoded by Claim
7.10
below.
∎

Claim 7.10
.

For all sufficiently small
\tau
,
\varepsilon>0
and all sufficiently large
C>0
depending on
\mathcal{C},\sigma
, we have

\displaystyle d_{g}(\operatorname{spt}\Sigma^{-},\operatorname{reg}_{>\tau\sigma}\Sigma^{+}\cap\partial B_{\tau}(p))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\cdot d_{g}(\operatorname{reg}_{>\tau\sigma}\Sigma^{-},\operatorname{reg}_{>\tau\sigma}\Sigma^{+}\cap(B_{2\tau}(p)\setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}\tau\sigma}(p)))

for all
p,p^{\pm},\Sigma^{\pm},\mathcal{C}^{\pm}
as in the statement of Proposition
7.9
.

Proof.

If this fails, we can find
\Sigma^{\pm}_{j},\mathcal{C}^{\pm}_{j},p^{\pm}_{j},p_{j}
, and
\tau_{j}\to 0
, so that
\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}^{\pm}_{j}}(\Sigma^{\pm}_{j};p^{\pm}_{j},\tau_{j})\to 0
but the assertion fails with
C=j
. By Lemmas
B.1
and
E.2
we find that
\iota_{p_{j}}^{-1}\;(\Sigma_{j}^{\pm})_{p_{j},\tau_{j}}
both converge to
\mathcal{C}
. Normalizing the difference of the graphs appropriately, we can use the Harnack inequality to find a positive Jacobi field
u
on
\mathcal{C}
with

\inf_{\operatorname{reg}_{>2\sigma}\mathcal{C}\cap\partial B_{1}}u\geq 1,

but

\inf_{\operatorname{reg}_{>\frac{1}{2}\sigma}\mathcal{C}\cap(B_{2}\setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}\sigma})}u=0.

This contradicts the Harnack inequality due to the connectedness of
\mathcal{C}
.
∎

7.5.
Main dichotomy

Recall the space
\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
of minimizing hypercones that are cylindrical over a quadratic hypercone from (
2.3
).

For the reasons outlined in the introduction, we will have to have different arguments to treat points modeled by
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
. Moreover, among such points, our arguments will further depend on the decay order of the approximating Jacobi field. Let us denote, for fixed
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-7\}
,

(7.17)

\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}}_{n-k}:=\min\{\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}},\;\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}\geq k\}\in(0,1],

where
\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}
is as in Remark
3.18
. Note that this is indeed a minimum since
\operatorname{Rot}(\cdot)
does not affect
\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}
, and
\mathscr{C}_{n-k}^{\textnormal{qd}}
is finite up to rotations. We also denote

(7.18)

\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}}:=\min\{\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}}_{n-k}:k\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-7\}\}\in(0,1],

This depends on
n
of course, but we omit this from the notation.

Lemma 7.11

(Dichotomy)
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}})
. There exist
\eta>0
,
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
depending on
n
,
\delta
with the following significance.

Suppose that
L_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
L_{0}\geq L
, and
p\in\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})
. Write
p\in\operatorname{sing}\Sigma
for
\Sigma\in\mathscr{F}
. Then, exactly one of the following holds:

(I)

There exists a unique
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
up to rotations, and a fixed copy
\mathcal{C}_{p}\subset T_{p}M
of it (independent of scale) such that:

\mathcal{N}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}}(\Sigma;p,2^{-\ell})>1+\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta\text{ for all }\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\;\ell\geq L_{0}.

(II)

There exists some
L_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
L_{1}\geq L_{0}
, such that for all
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
:

\bar{\mathcal{N}}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Sigma;p,2^{-\ell})\leq 1+\delta\text{ for all }\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\;\ell\geq L_{1}.

Proof.

Note that up to rotations
\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
is a finite set. The statement then follows from Corollary
4.11
and that
\delta<\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}}
.
∎

Remark 7.12
.

We emphasize that in case (II) of Lemma
7.11
we have no control over how large
L_{1}\geq L_{0}
may need to be, so we need to consider all possibilities.

Definition 7.13
.

Let
\delta
,
\eta
,
L
be as in Lemma
7.11
. Take any
L_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
L_{0}\geq L
.

(I)

Denote
\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})\subset\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})
the set of
p
for which Lemma
7.11
(I) holds with
\eta
,
L_{0}
, and a copy
\mathcal{C}_{p}\subset T_{p}M
of some
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
, i.e.:

\mathcal{N}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}}(\Sigma;p,2^{-\ell})>1+\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta\text{ for all }\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\;\ell\geq L_{0}.

(II)

Denote
\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})\subset\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})
the set of
p
for which Lemma
7.11
(II) holds with
\eta
and some
L_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
L_{1}\geq L_{0}
, i.e., for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
:

\bar{\mathcal{N}}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Sigma;p,2^{-\ell})\leq 1+\delta\text{ for all }\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\;\ell\geq L_{1}.

An immediate consequence of Lemma
7.11
and Definition
7.13
is that:

(7.19)

\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})=\left(\bigcup_{\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}}\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})\right)\cup\left(\bigcup_{L_{1}\geq L_{0}}\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})\right).

Remark 7.14
.

The first union, over
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
, is really a finite union since
\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})
is independent of rotations of
\mathcal{C}
.

7.6.
Points in
\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})

Fix
\delta\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}})
(see (
7.18
)),
\sigma\in(0,\min\{\sigma_{0}(n),\sigma_{1}(n)\})
(see Proposition
7.9
and Lemma
C.1
), and some
\theta=2^{-m}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, to be determined.

Given these, we take
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(n,\sigma,\theta)>0
,
\eta=\eta(n,\delta,\sigma,\theta)>0
,
L=L(n,\delta,\sigma,\theta)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
so that Proposition
7.1
, Lemmas
7.4
,
7.11
, Proposition
7.9
, and Corollary
4.11
all hold for all
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
(there is only a discrete choice of
\mathcal{C}
, see Remark
7.14
).

Fix some
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
and
L_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
L_{0}\geq L
. Then Corollary
4.11
and Lemma
7.11
(I) imply that there exists

L_{2}=L_{2}(n,L_{0},\varepsilon)\geq L_{0}

so that:

(7.20)

\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}}(\Sigma;p,2^{-\ell})<\varepsilon\text{ for all }\ell\geq L_{2},

where
p\in\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})
,
p\in\operatorname{sing}\Sigma
,
\Sigma\in\mathscr{F}
, and some copy
\mathcal{C}_{p}\subset T_{p}M
of
\mathcal{C}
.

We proceed to construct iterative covers
\mathfrak{C}_{j}
of
\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})
by subsets of radius-
2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}
balls in a manner compatible with Proposition
6.1
. Each element

B_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}_{j}

will be constructed to be an intersection of a chain of balls with geometrically decreasing radii:

B_{j}=\bigcap_{i=0}^{j}B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{i}}(p_{i});

cf. Lemma
7.5
’s (2) for why this format is useful.

The construction of the various
\mathfrak{C}_{j}
and the parent map
\mathfrak{p}:\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}\to\mathfrak{C}_{j}
is as follows:

•

For the base case,
j=0
, take any Vitali cover

\mathfrak{C}_{0}=\{B_{2^{-L_{2}}}(p_{0})\}_{p_{0}}\text{ of }\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})

using radius-
2^{-L_{2}}
balls centered at various points
p_{0}\in\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})
.

•

For the inductive step, consider
j\geq 0
and any already constructed

B_{j}:=\bigcap_{i=0}^{j}B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{i}}(p_{i}).

Then, take any Vitali cover

\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}[B_{j}]=\{B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j+1}}(p_{j+1})\}_{p_{j+1}}\text{ of }B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})

using radius-
2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j+1}
balls centered at various points
p_{j+1}\in B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})
. For each such
p_{j+1}
, consider the corresponding

B_{j+1}:=B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j+1}}(p_{j+1})\cap B_{j}=\bigcap_{i=0}^{j+1}B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{i}}(p_{i}).

If it’s empty or has already occurred at the
j
-th step, discard it. Otherwise, set

\mathfrak{p}(B_{j+1}):=B_{j}.

•

Once all
B_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}_{j}
have been considered and their
\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}[B_{j}]
has been constructed as above, define

\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}:=\bigcup_{B_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}_{j}}\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}[B_{j}].

We now seek to establish the quantitative properties for
\mathfrak{C}_{j}
required by Proposition
6.1
(3) and (4). To that end, revisit the inductive construction starting from

B_{j}=\bigcap_{i=0}^{j}B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{i}}(p_{i}).

In view of (
7.20
), we know that Proposition
7.1
applies for
p_{j}\in\operatorname{sing}\Sigma
,
\Sigma\in\mathscr{F}
, at scale
2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}
with some “a priori” hypercone
\mathcal{C}(B_{j})
, and yields some “a posteriori” hypercone
\check{\mathcal{C}}(B_{j})
, and that both hypercones are copies of our fixed
\mathcal{C}
by (iv) of the proposition.

By (i) of Proposition
7.1
,

B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})\subset\eta_{p_{j},2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}}(B_{\theta}(\operatorname{spine}\check{\mathcal{C}}(B_{j}))).

Since
\dim\operatorname{spine}\check{\mathcal{C}}(B_{j})=\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}=k
, our curvature bounds on
(M,g)
allow us to estimate the cardinality of the Vitali cover by

(7.21)

\#\mathfrak{p}^{-1}(B_{j})=\#\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}[B_{j}]\leq C_{1}^{\prime}\cdot\theta^{-k}

for a geometric constant
C_{1}^{\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}(n)
.

Next, we claim that, for every
p^{\pm}\in B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})
,

(7.22)

|\mathfrak{T}(p^{+})-\mathfrak{T}(p^{-})|\leq(\operatorname{Lip}\mathfrak{T})\cdot 2^{-L_{2}}\cdot C_{2}^{\prime}\cdot H^{j}\cdot\theta^{j(1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)+\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta)},

with
C_{2}^{\prime}=C_{2}^{\prime}(n,\sigma)>0
. We defer the proof of (
7.22
) until the section’s end. (It follows by combining Lemma
7.5
with Proposition
7.9
and Corollary
4.11
.) Taking (
7.22
) for granted and then taking suprema over
p^{\pm}\in B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})
:

(7.23)

\operatorname{diam}\mathfrak{T}(B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0}))\leq C_{2}\cdot(C_{1}^{\prime\prime})^{j}\cdot\theta^{j(1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)+\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta)},

for
C_{2}=C_{2}(n,\sigma,L_{2},\operatorname{Lip}\mathfrak{T})>0
and
C_{1}^{\prime\prime}=H(n,\sigma)>0
.

Fix
C_{1}=\max\{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\}
and
C_{2}
as above. Then, (
7.21
), (
7.23
) imply that Proposition
6.1
’s (3), (4) are satisfied with

\mathfrak{D}(B_{j}):=k,

\mathfrak{h}(B_{j}):=1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)+\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta,

for all
B_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}_{j}
,
j=0,1,2,\ldots
Thus, Proposition
6.1
implies:

Corollary 7.15
.

Fix
\xi>0
,
\delta\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}})
(see Lemma
7.11
) and
\sigma\in(0,\min\{\sigma_{0}(n),\sigma_{1}(n)\})
(see Proposition
7.9
and Lemma
C.1
). Then, there exist
\eta>0
,
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
depending on
n,\delta,\sigma,\xi
so that, for all
L_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
L_{0}\geq L
,

(7.24)

\dim\mathfrak{T}(\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0}))\leq\frac{k+\xi}{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)+\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta},

and, for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

(7.25)

\dim(\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})\cap\mathfrak{T}^{-1}(t))\leq\max\{k-(1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)+\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta),0\}+\xi.

We emphasize that, above,
k=\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-7\}
.

Proof.

Our choice of
\sigma
fixes
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,\sigma)
in (
7.23
).

We may then fix
\theta=\theta(n,\sigma,\xi)
of the form
2^{-m}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, so that Proposition
6.1
applies with this
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,\sigma)
and our chosen
\xi
.

Then take
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(n,\sigma,\xi)>0
so that Propositions
7.1
and
7.9
both hold, and
\eta=\eta(n,\delta,\sigma,\xi)>0
,
L=L(n,\delta,\sigma,\xi)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
so that Proposition
7.1
, Lemmas
7.4
,
7.11
, and Corollary
4.11
all apply.

Then, for all
L_{0}\geq L
, the coverings we constructed precisely fit into Proposition
6.1
, which yields the required result.
∎

Proof of (
7.22
).

Corollary
4.11
(ii) implies the following distance to the unique tangent cones
\mathcal{C}_{p^{\pm}}
at
p^{\pm}\in\operatorname{sing}\Sigma^{\pm}
:

(7.26)

\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{C}_{p^{\pm}}}(\Sigma^{\pm};p^{\pm},2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j})\leq C(n)\cdot(2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}\cdot 2^{L_{0}})^{(\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta)}\leq C(n)\cdot\theta^{j(\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta)},

where we used
L_{2}\geq L_{0}
. We may plug (
7.26
) into Proposition
7.9
to obtain:

\displaystyle d_{j}

\displaystyle:=\frac{d_{g}(\operatorname{spt}\Sigma^{-},\operatorname{reg}_{>2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}\sigma}\Sigma^{+}\cap\partial B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}}(p_{j}))}{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\cdot\left(2\cdot C(n)\cdot\theta^{j(\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta)}+(2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j})^{2}\right)

(7.27)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}^{\prime}\cdot\theta^{j(\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta)},

with
C_{2}^{\prime}=C_{2}^{\prime}(n,\sigma)>0
. The result follows from Lemma
7.5
(ii) applied with
\ell=L_{2}
(recall that
\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})\subset\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{2})
by Remark
7.3
), with (
7.6
) used to bound
d_{j}
, and with Proposition
7.1
(iv) to see that the
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
are rotations of our fixed
\mathcal{C}
.
∎

7.7.
Points in
\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})

Fix
\sigma\in(0,\sigma_{1}(n))
(see Lemma
C.1
) and some
\theta=2^{-m}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, to be determined.

Now take
\delta=\delta(n,\theta)\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}})
(see (
7.18
)),
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(n,\sigma,\theta)>0
,
\eta=\eta(n,\sigma,\theta)>0
,
L=L(n,\sigma,\theta)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
so that Proposition
7.1
, Lemmas
7.4
,
7.11
, and Theorem
4.13
all hold for all
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
. In what follows, we work with
L_{0},L_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, with

L_{1}\geq L_{0}\geq L.

and fix

L_{2}:=L_{1}+L.

We proceed to construct iterative covers
\mathfrak{C}_{j}
of
\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})
by subsets of radius-
2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}
balls in a manner compatible with Proposition
6.1
. The process is like the one in the previous section. However, rather than improve the coarse Hölder exponents (by improving Lemma
7.5
(ii) using Proposition
7.9
), we will improve the coarse dimensions (by improving Proposition
7.1
(ii) using Theorem
4.13
). The construction of the various
\mathfrak{C}_{j}
and the parent map
\mathfrak{p}:\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}\to\mathfrak{C}_{j}
is as follows:

•

For the base case,
j=0
, take any Vitali cover

\mathfrak{C}_{0}=\{B_{2^{-L_{2}}}(p_{0})\}_{p_{0}}\text{ of }\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})

using radius-
2^{-L_{2}}
balls centered at various points
p_{0}\in\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})
.

•

For the inductive step, consider
j\geq 0
and any already constructed

B_{j}:=\bigcap_{i=0}^{j}B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{i}}(p_{i}).

Then, take any Vitali cover

\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}[B_{j}]=\{B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j+1}}(p_{j+1})\}_{p_{j+1}}\text{ of }B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})

using radius-
2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j+1}
balls centered at various points
p_{j+1}\in B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})
. For each such
p_{j+1}
, consider the corresponding

B_{j+1}:=B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j+1}}(p_{j+1})\cap B_{j}=\bigcap_{i=0}^{j+1}B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{i}}(p_{i}).

If it’s empty or has already occurred at the
j
-th step, discard it. Otherwise, set

\mathfrak{p}(B_{j+1}):=B_{j}.

•

Once all
B_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}_{j}
have been considered and their
\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}[B_{j}]
has been constructed as above, define

\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}:=\bigcup_{B_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}_{j}}\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}[B_{j}].

We now establish the quantitative properties for
\mathfrak{C}_{j}
required by Proposition
6.1
(3) and (4). To that end, revisit the inductive construction starting from

B_{j}=\bigcap_{i=0}^{j}B_{2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{i}}(p_{i}).

In view of Lemma
7.4
, we know that Proposition
7.1
applies for
p_{j}\in\operatorname{sing}\Sigma
,
\Sigma\in\mathscr{F}
, at scale
2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}
with some “a priori” hypercone
\mathcal{C}(B_{j})
, and yields some “a posteriori” hypercone
\check{\mathcal{C}}(B_{j})
. These need not be congruent hypercones anymore.

It follows from Proposition
7.1
(i) and Theorem
4.13
that

B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})\subset\eta_{p_{j},2^{-L_{2}}\theta^{j}}(B_{\theta}(V(B_{j})))

where
V(B_{j})
is a linear subspace determined as follows:

•

If
\mathcal{C}(B_{j})
is a hypercopy of a cone in
\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
, then by Theorem
4.13

V(B_{j})
can be taken to be a proper linear subspace of
\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}(B_{j})
, i.e.,

\dim V(B_{j})\leq\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}(B_{j})-1=\dim\operatorname{spine}\check{\mathcal{C}}(B_{j})-1.

The last equality is from Proposition
7.1
(iv).

•

In all other cases,
V(B_{j})=\operatorname{spine}\check{\mathcal{C}}(B_{j})
by Proposition
7.1
, so

\dim V(B_{j})=\dim\operatorname{spine}\check{\mathcal{C}}(B_{j}).

Our curvature bounds on
(M,g)
allow us to estimate

(7.28)

\#\mathfrak{p}^{-1}(B_{j})=\#\mathfrak{C}_{j+1}[B_{j}]\leq C_{1}^{\prime}\cdot\theta^{-\dim V(B_{j})}

for a geometric constant
C_{1}^{\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}(n)
.

It follows from Lemma
7.5
(i), (ii) and taking suprema over
B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})
that

(7.29)

\operatorname{diam}\mathfrak{T}(B_{j}\cap\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0}))\leq C_{2}\cdot(C_{1}^{\prime\prime})^{j}\cdot\theta^{\sum_{i=1}^{j}(1+\alpha(\check{\mathcal{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{i}(B_{j}));\sigma))},

for
C_{2}=C_{2}(n,L_{2},\operatorname{Lip}\mathfrak{T})>0
and
C_{1}^{\prime\prime}=H(n,\sigma)>0
.

Fix
C_{1}=\max\{C_{1}^{\prime},C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\}
and
C_{2}
as above. Then, (
7.28
), (
7.29
) imply that Proposition
6.1
’s (3), (4) are satisfied with

\mathfrak{D}(B_{j}):=\dim V(B_{j}),

\mathfrak{h}(B_{j}):=1+\alpha(\check{\mathcal{C}}(B_{j});\sigma),

for all
B_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}_{j}
,
j=0,1,2,\ldots
Thus, Proposition
6.1
implies:

Corollary 7.16
.

Fix
\xi>0
,
\sigma\in(0,\sigma_{1}(n))
(see Lemma
C.1
). For all sufficiently small
\delta>0
depending on
n,\sigma,\xi
, there exist
\eta>0
,
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
depending on
n,\sigma,\delta,\xi
so that for all
L_{0},L_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
L_{1}\geq L_{0}\geq L
,

\displaystyle\dim\mathfrak{T}(\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0}))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\max\Big\{\max_{\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}}\Big\{\frac{\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}-1+\xi}{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)}\Big\},

(7.30)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\sup_{\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}\setminus\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}}\Big\{\frac{\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}+\xi}{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)}\Big\}\Big\},

and, for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\dim(\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})\cap\mathfrak{T}^{-1}(t))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\max\Big\{\max_{\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}}\Big\{\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}-(2+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma))\Big\},

(7.31)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\sup_{\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}\setminus\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}}\Big\{\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}-(1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma))\Big\},0\Big\}+\xi.

Proof.

Our choice of
\sigma
forces a
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,\sigma)
as defined after (
7.29
).

We may then fix
\theta=\theta(n,\sigma,\xi)
of the form
2^{-m}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, so that Proposition
6.1
applies with this
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,\sigma)
and our chosen
\xi
.

Having fixed
\theta=\theta(n,\sigma,\xi)
, our smallness requirement on
\delta
is:

•

We allow any
\delta=\delta(n,\theta)=\delta(n,\sigma,\xi)>0
so that Lemma
7.11
and Theorem
4.13
apply.

Then take
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(n,\sigma,\xi)>0
so that Proposition
7.1
and Theorem
4.13
hold, and
\eta=\eta(n,\sigma,\delta,\xi)>0
,
L=L(n,\sigma,\delta,\xi)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
so that Proposition
7.1
, Lemmas
7.4
,
7.11
, and Theorem
4.13
all apply.

Then, for all
L_{0},L_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
L_{1}\geq L_{0}\geq L
, the coverings we constructed for
\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})
precisely fit into Proposition
6.1
. Moreover, for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
, we saw how to obtain the asserted 1-dimensional improvement in
\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}
.
∎

7.8.
Proof of Theorem
1.4

(i) Note that
\dim\mathfrak{T}(\operatorname{sing}\mathscr{F})\leq 1
is automatic since
\mathfrak{T}
is
\mathbb{R}
-valued, so it suffices to just prove
\dim\mathfrak{T}(\operatorname{sing}\mathscr{F})\leq{\mathfrak{d}_{n}^{\textnormal{img}}}
.

Recall from the remark after Definition
7.2
that, for any
\eta>0
,

L_{0}\mapsto\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})

is a monotone exhaustion of
\operatorname{sing}\mathscr{F}
. In particular, since Hausdorff dimension bounds are preserved under countable unions, it suffices to prove

(7.32)

\dim\mathfrak{T}(\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0}))\leq{\mathfrak{d}_{n}^{\textnormal{img}}}

for a fixed
\eta>0
, and all large enough
L_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Let
\xi>0
be arbitrary. Fix
\epsilon>0
so that Proposition
5.7
(i) and (iii) apply with this choice of
\xi
. Then choose
\sigma>0
sufficiently small so that Proposition
5.7
(ii) also applies with this choice of
\epsilon
, and so do Corollaries
7.15
,
7.16
with these
\sigma,\xi
. Finally, choose any
\delta>0
small enough that Lemma
7.11

and Corollaries
7.15
,
7.16
holds.

Lemma
7.11
and Corollaries
7.15
,
7.16
then yield
\eta>0
,
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Choose any
L_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
where
L_{0}\geq L
. By our previous discussion, it suffices to prove (
7.32
) with this
L_{0}
. To that end, we use the decomposition of
\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})
from (
7.19
), i.e.,

(7.33)

\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})=\left(\bigcup_{\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}}\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})\right)\cup\left(\bigcup_{L_{1}\geq L_{0}}\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})\right).

We now observe that Corollary
7.15
can bound, for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
with
\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}=k
,

\displaystyle\dim\mathfrak{T}(\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k+\xi}{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)+\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}-\delta}

(7.34)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-k}+\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}}_{n-k}-\delta-\xi}.

The first inequality is merely Corollary
7.15
’s (
7.24
). For the second inequality we used Proposition
5.7
(i) to estimate
\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)>\alpha_{n-k}-\xi
and (
7.17
) to estimate
\Delta^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}\geq\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}}_{n-k}
. Taking unions over all
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
(finitely many up to rotation, see Remark
7.14
):

(7.35)

\dim\mathfrak{T}\left(\bigcup_{\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}}\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})\right)\leq\max_{k=0,1,\ldots,n-7}\Big\{\frac{k+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-k}+\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}}_{n-k}-\delta-\xi}\Big\}.

We also observe that Corollary
7.16
can bound, for all
L_{1}\geq L_{0}
,

\displaystyle\dim\mathfrak{T}(\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0}))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\max\Big\{\max_{\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}}\Big\{\frac{\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}-1+\xi}{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)}\Big\},\sup_{\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}\setminus\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}}\Big\{\frac{\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}+\xi}{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)}\Big\}\Big\}

(7.36)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\max_{k=0,1,\ldots,n-7}\Big\{\frac{k-1+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-k}-\xi},\frac{k+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-k}+\Delta_{n-k}^{\textnormal{non-qd}}-\xi}\Big\}.

The first inequality is again merely Corollary
7.16
’s (
7.16
). For the second inequality, need a combination of arguments.

•

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
with
\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}=k
, Proposition
5.7
’s (i) estimates

\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)>\alpha_{n-k}-\xi,

so

\frac{\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}-1+\xi}{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)}\leq\frac{k-1+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-k}-\xi},

which is present in the final maximum expression.

•

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}\setminus\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
with
\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}=k
which
is not

\epsilon
-close to
\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
in the metric sense of Proposition
5.7
, conclusion (ii) of Proposition
5.7
estimates

\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)>\alpha_{n-k}+\Delta_{n-k}^{\textnormal{non-qd}}-\xi,

so

\frac{\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}+\xi}{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)}\leq\frac{k+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-k}+\Delta_{n-k}^{\textnormal{non-qd}}-\xi},

which is present in the final maximum expression.

•

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}\setminus\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
with
\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}=k
which
is

\epsilon
-close to
\mathscr{C}_{n}^{\textnormal{qd-cyl}}
in the metric sense of Proposition
5.7
, conclusion (iii) of Proposition
5.7
guarantees that
\mathcal{C}
is
\epsilon
-close to a cone
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}_{n}
with
k^{\prime}:=\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\geq k+1
. Then, conclusion (i) of Proposition
5.7
estimates

\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)>\alpha_{n-(k+1)}-\xi,

so

\frac{\dim\operatorname{spine}\mathcal{C}+\xi}{1+\alpha(\mathcal{C};\sigma)}\leq\frac{k+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-(k+1)}-\xi}=\frac{(k+1)-1+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-(k+1)}-\xi},

which is present in the final maximum expression (since
k+1\leq k^{\prime}\leq n-7
).

Now that we have justified (
7.8
), we recall Definition
7.13
and take countable unions over
L_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
L_{1}\geq L_{0}
to obtain:

\displaystyle\dim\mathfrak{T}\left(\bigcup_{L_{1}\geq L_{0}}\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})\right)

(7.37)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\max_{k=0,1,\ldots,n-7}\Big\{\frac{k-1+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-k}-\xi},\frac{k+\xi}{1+\alpha_{n-k}+\Delta_{n-k}^{\textnormal{non-qd}}-\xi}\Big\}.

Together, (
7.33
), (
7.35
), (
7.8
) and the definition of
{\mathfrak{d}_{n}^{\textnormal{img}}}
in (
1.1
) imply:

\dim\mathfrak{T}(\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0}))\leq{\mathfrak{d}_{n}^{\textnormal{img}}}+\Psi(\xi,\delta),

where
\Psi(a,b)
denotes an expression which
\to 0
as
|a|+|b|\to 0
. Sending the arbitrary
L_{0}\to\infty
, and recalling Remark
7.3
, we deduce that

\dim\mathfrak{T}(\operatorname{sing}\mathscr{F})=\dim\mathfrak{T}\left(\bigcup_{L_{0}}\mathcal{S}(\eta,L_{0})\right)\leq{\mathfrak{d}_{n}^{\textnormal{img}}}+\Psi(\xi,\delta).

The result now follows by sending
\delta\to 0
,
\xi\to 0
.

(ii) The proof is essentially the same, so we describe the modifications. Instead of (
7.8
), we prove that, for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\dim(\mathcal{S}^{>1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta,L_{0})\cap\mathfrak{T}^{-1}(t))\leq\max\{k-(1+\alpha_{n-k}+\Delta^{\textnormal{qd}}_{n-k}-\delta-\xi),0\}+\xi.

by using Corollary
7.15
’s (
7.25
) instead of (
7.24
). Instead of (
7.8
), we prove that for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\dim(\mathcal{S}^{=1}_{L_{1}}(\eta,L_{0})\cap\mathfrak{T}^{-1}(t))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\max_{k=0,1,\ldots,n-7}\{k-(2+\alpha_{n-k}-\xi),k-(1+\alpha_{n-k}+\Delta_{n-k}^{\textnormal{non-qd}}-\xi)\},0\Big\}+\xi.

by using Corollary
7.16
’s (
7.16
) instead of (
7.16
). Then, since
\Delta_{n-k}^{\textnormal{qd}}\in(0,1]
, it follows from these two inequalities and the definition of

in (

) that for a.e.
