\Sigma
satisfies
\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\operatorname{dist}}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
then there exists an open subset of
\mathcal{C}
and a
C^{2}
function
u
on it with

(H.15)

\Sigma\cap(Q_{1}\setminus\{r<1/4\})=\operatorname{graph}_{\mathcal{C}}u,\qquad\|u\|_{C^{2}_{*}}<\kappa_{\mathcal{C}}.

We fix these
\kappa_{\mathcal{C}}
,
\varepsilon_{0}(\mathcal{C}),\delta_{0}(\mathcal{C})
for the rest of the appendix.

The following lemma is a modified version of
[
Sim94
, Lemma 1.4]
. We first introduce the following notation. Let
Z\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
be a closed subset and
h:Z\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be an upper-semi-continuous function. Denote by

(H.16)

\bar{B}_{h}=\bar{B}_{h}(Z):=\bigcup_{z\in Z}\bar{B}_{h(z)}(z)\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\,,

which is also a closed subset (note, for
h(z)=0
,
\bar{B}_{h(z)}(z)
is a point). We will also need to work with annuli

A^{n_{\circ}+1}_{s_{1},s_{2}}=B^{n_{\circ}+1}_{s_{2}}\setminus\bar{B}^{n_{\circ}+1}_{s_{1}}

in the first
\mathbb{R}^{n_{\circ}+1}
factor.

Note that since
\Sigma
is mininal with respect to
g
, its mean curvature with respect to
\bar{g}
is controlled by
C\delta r^{-1}_{\Sigma}
.

Lemma H.6
.

Let
\alpha>4-n_{\circ}
. There exist
\varepsilon_{1}(\mathcal{C})\leq\varepsilon_{0}(\mathcal{C})
,
\delta_{1}(\mathcal{C},\alpha)\leq\delta_{0}(\mathcal{C})
,
C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)
with the following property. Let
\delta\leq\delta_{1}
in (
H.1
) and
\Sigma
be a
g
-minimizing boundary in
B_{2}
with
\operatorname{spt}\Sigma\cap B_{1}\neq\emptyset
and
\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\operatorname{dist}}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\,d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
. Then there exists an upper-semi-continuous
s_{\Sigma}:\bar{B}_{1}^{k}\to[0,1/20)
and
u
defined on an open subset of
\mathcal{C}
such that

\operatorname{graph}_{\mathcal{C}}u=\Sigma\cap U\text{ with }\|u\|_{C^{2}_{*}}<\kappa_{\mathcal{C}}

where

U:=Q_{1}\setminus\bar{B}_{s_{\Sigma}}(\{0\}\times\bar{B}_{1}^{k})\supset A_{1/2,1}^{n_{\circ}+1}\times B_{1}^{k}\,.

Moreover, for
1/2\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq 1
, the following estimate holds if
\delta\leq\delta_{1}
in (
H.1
):

\begin{split}\int_{Q_{\tau_{1}}\cap\Dom(u)}r^{\alpha}(|\nabla u|^{2}+r^{-2}u^{2})\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}+\int_{Q_{\tau_{1}}\cap\bar{B}_{2s_{\Sigma}}(\{0\}\times B_{1}^{k})}r^{\alpha}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
\leq\ \frac{C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{\alpha-2}\overline{\operatorname{dist}}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}+C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)\cdot\delta^{2}\,.\end{split}

Proof.

For each
y\in\bar{B}^{k}_{1}
, we define

s_{\Sigma}(y):=\inf\big\{s\in(0,1/2):\forall s^{\prime}\in[s,1/2]\textrm{ we have }\Sigma\cap(A^{n_{\circ}+1}_{s^{\prime},{2s^{\prime}}}\times B_{2s^{\prime}}^{k}(y))=\operatorname{graph}_{\mathcal{C}}u\\
\text{for some function $u$ defined on an open subset of }\mathcal{C}\text{ with }\|u\|_{C^{2}_{*}}<\kappa_{\mathcal{C}}\big\}\,.

It is clear that
s_{\Sigma}
is upper-semi-continuous in
y
. By taking
\varepsilon_{1},\delta_{1}
even smaller, we may assume
s_{\Sigma}\leq 1/20
. By rescaling, a compactness argument, unique continuation and interior estimates for the minimal surface equation, we always have for every
y\in{B}_{\tau_{1}}^{k}\cap\{s_{\Sigma}>0\}
,

(H.17)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}\cap(A^{n_{\circ}+1}_{s_{\Sigma}(y),2s_{\Sigma}(y)}\times B^{k}_{2s_{\Sigma}(y)}(y))}r^{\alpha-2}\overline{\operatorname{dist}}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}+\delta^{2}\cdot s_{\Sigma}(y)^{n+\alpha}\geq C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)^{-1}s_{\Sigma}(y)^{n+\alpha}\,.

The monotonicity formula yields for every
y\in{B}_{\tau_{1}}^{k}\cap\{s_{\Sigma}>0\}
,

(H.18)

\int_{Q_{\tau_{1}}\cap B_{10s_{\Sigma}(y)}(0,y)}r^{\alpha}\,d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\leq C(n,\alpha)s_{\Sigma}(y)^{n+\alpha}\,,

so when
\delta\leq\delta_{1}(\mathcal{C},\alpha)
, by combining (
H.17
) and (
H.18
) with the Vitali covering lemma we obtain

\int_{Q_{\tau_{1}}\cap\bar{B}_{2s_{\Sigma}}}r^{\alpha}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\ \leq\ C(C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)^{-1}-\delta^{2})^{-1}\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{\alpha-2}\overline{\operatorname{dist}}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\,,

where
\bar{B}_{2s_{\Sigma}}
is defined via (
H.16
) with
Z=\bar{B}^{k}_{1}
. Using
\|u\|_{C^{2}_{*}}<\kappa_{\mathcal{C}}
on
\Dom(u)
, a similar argument gives

(H.19)

\int_{Q_{\tau_{1}}\cap\Dom(u)\cap\overline{B}_{2s_{\Sigma}}}r^{\alpha}(|\nabla u|^{2}+r^{-2}u^{2})\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\\
\leq\ C(C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)^{-1}-\delta^{2})^{-1}\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{\alpha-2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}.

On the other hand, for any
(x,y)\in(Q_{\tau_{1}}\cap\Dom(u))\setminus\overline{B}_{2s_{\Sigma}}
, it follows from the definition of
s_{\Sigma}
that there exists a universal
c>1
such that

Q_{|x|/c}(x,y)\subset\Dom(u),

Q_{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})|x|/c}\subset Q_{\tau_{2}}.

Interior estimates for the minimal surface equation yield

\int_{Q_{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})|x|/2c}(x,y)}(r^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2})\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\\
\leq C\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\cdot\int_{Q_{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})|x|/c}(x,y)}u^{2}\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}+C(\mathcal{C})\cdot|x|^{n-2}\cdot\delta^{2}.

Since
r
and
|x|
are comparable on
Q_{|x|/c}(x,y)
, i.e.,
C^{-1}r\leq|x|\leq Cr
for a
C>1
(which depends on
c
), the previous estimate directly implies:

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})|x|/2c}(x,y)}r^{\alpha}(|\nabla u|^{2}+r^{-2}u^{2})\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C^{\alpha-2}\cdot|x|^{\alpha-2}\cdot\int_{Q_{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})|x|/2c}(x,y)}(r^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2})\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C^{\alpha-2}\cdot|x|^{\alpha-2}\cdot\left(\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\int_{Q_{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})|x|/2c}(x,y)}u^{2}d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}+C(\mathcal{C})\cdot|x|^{n-2}\cdot\delta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\int_{Q_{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})|x|/c}(x,y)}r^{\alpha-2}u^{2}\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad+C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)\cdot\left(\int_{Q_{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})|x|/c}(x,y)}r^{\alpha-4}\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\right)\cdot\delta^{2}.

Using the Besicovitch covering lemma we find that

(H.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{Q_{\tau_{1}}\cap\Dom(u)\setminus\overline{B}_{2s_{\Sigma}}}r^{\alpha}(|\nabla u|^{2}+r^{-2}u^{2})\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\leq\ C\frac{C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}\cap\Dom(u)}r^{\alpha-2}u^{2}\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}+C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)\cdot\left(\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{\alpha-4}\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\right)\cdot\delta^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq\ \frac{C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{\alpha-2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}+C(\mathcal{C},\alpha)\cdot\delta^{2}.\end{split}

In the last step, we used the fact that
u
is comparable to
\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}
and
d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}
to
d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}
on
\Dom(u)
and that
\alpha>4-n_{\circ}
guarantees the integrability of
r^{\alpha-4}
on
\mathcal{C}
. The assertion thus follows by combining estimates (
H.19
) and (
H.20
).
∎

Below we fix
\varepsilon_{1}(\mathcal{C}),\delta_{1}(\mathcal{C},1)>0
as in Lemma
H.6
with
\alpha=1
.

Corollary H.7
.

There exists
C(\mathcal{C})>0
with the following significance.

If
\delta\leq\delta_{1}
in (
H.1
),
\Sigma
is a
g
-minimizing boundary in
B_{2}
with
\operatorname{spt}\Sigma\cap B_{1}\neq\emptyset
and
\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\operatorname{dist}}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\,d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
,
1/2\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq 1
,
\tau_{3}=(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2})/2
,
\zeta\in C_{c}^{1}(B_{\tau_{3}}^{k})
,
\psi\in C_{c}^{2}([0,\tau_{3}))
, and
\psi|_{[0,\tau_{1}]}\equiv 1
, then

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\ (d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}-d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\cdot(\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{2}})\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-1}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}+C(\mathcal{C})\cdot(\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{2}})\cdot\delta\,.

Proof.

Take
f=1
and
\varphi=\psi
in Lemma
H.5
we get

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)(n_{\circ}+|\nu_{y}|^{2})\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\underbrace{\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\big(|\nabla_{\bar{g}}\zeta(y)|\psi(r)|\nu_{y}||r\partial_{r}^{\perp}|+\zeta(y)|\psi^{\prime}(r)|r|\partial_{r}^{\perp}|^{2}\big)\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}}_{\text{(I)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi^{\prime}(r)r\ \big(d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}-d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\big)}_{\text{(II)}}-\underbrace{\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi^{\prime}(r)r\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}}_{\text{(III)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C(\mathcal{C})\cdot\delta\cdot\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}}\|\psi\|_{C^{1}}.

To deal with (III), notice that if we apply Lemma
H.5
with
\Sigma=\mathcal{C}
,
g=\bar{g}
,
f=1
and
\zeta,\psi
as above, then since
|\nu_{y}|=|\partial_{r}^{\perp}|=0
, we get

(H.21)

\displaystyle\text{(III)}=-n_{\circ}\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\,.

To deal with (I), recall that
\operatorname{spt}(\zeta(y)\psi(r))\subset Q_{\tau_{3}}
. Let
U
be the domain given by Lemma
H.6
with
\Sigma\cap U=\operatorname{graph}_{\mathcal{C}}u
. We have

(H.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{(I)}&\leq 2\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}}r(|\partial_{r}^{\perp}|+|\nu_{y}|)^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\leq 2\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\left(\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}\cap U}r(|\partial_{r}^{\perp}|+|\nu_{y}|)^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}+\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}\setminus U}4r\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\right)\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{C})\cdot\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\left(\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}\cap\Dom(u)}r|\nabla u|^{2}\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}+\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}\setminus U}r\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\cdot\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-1}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}+C(\mathcal{C})\cdot\|\zeta\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\delta^{2}\end{split}

where the third inequality follows from (
H.11
) and (
H.12
) by parametrizing
\Sigma\cap U=\operatorname{graph}_{\mathcal{C}}u
, and the last inequality follows from Lemma
H.6
with
\alpha=1
and
\tau_{3}
in place of
\tau_{1}
.

To deal with (II), recall that
\operatorname{spt}(\psi^{\prime})\subset\{\tau_{1}\leq r\leq\tau_{3}\}
, and that by (
H.15
) we know that
\Sigma
is a
\kappa_{\mathcal{C}}
-
C^{2}_{*}
graph of some function
u
over
\mathcal{C}
in
\operatorname{spt}(\psi^{\prime})
. Therefore, if
F(x,z,p)
denotes the corresponding Jacobian factor of the graph,

(H.23)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{(II)}&=\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}}\zeta(y)\left(r\psi^{\prime}(r)-\sqrt{r^{2}+u^{2}}\psi^{\prime}(\sqrt{r^{2}+u^{2}})F(x,u,\nabla u)\right)\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{C})\cdot\|\zeta\|_{C^{0}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{2}}\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}\cap\Dom(u)}r(|\nabla u|^{2}+r^{-2}u^{2})\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\leq\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\cdot\|\zeta\|_{C^{0}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{2}}\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-1}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}+C(\mathcal{C})\cdot\delta^{2}\cdot\|\zeta\|_{C^{0}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{2}}.\end{split}

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
H.6
with
\alpha=1
and
\tau_{3}
in place of
\tau_{1}
. Combining (
H.21
), (
H.22
) and (
H.23
) proves the Corollary.
∎

Lemma H.8
.

There exists
C(\mathcal{C})>0
with the following significance.

If
\delta\leq\delta_{1}
in (
H.1
) and
\Sigma
is
g
-minimizing boundary in
B_{2}
with
\operatorname{spt}\Sigma\cap B_{1}\neq\emptyset
and
\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\operatorname{dist}}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\,d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
and
1/2\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq 1
, then

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\tau_{1}}}r^{-2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{5}}\cdot\left(\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-1}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}+\delta\right)

Proof.

Let
\tau_{3}:=(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2})/2
. Choose

\zeta\in C^{2}_{c}(B^{k}_{\tau_{3}}),\;\zeta|_{B^{k}_{\tau_{1}}}=1,\;0\leq\zeta\leq 1,

\psi\in C_{c}^{2}([0,\tau_{3})),\;\psi|_{[0,\tau_{1}]}=1,\;0\leq\psi\leq 1.

Note that we can assume that

\|\psi\|_{C^{l}}+\|\zeta\|_{C^{l}}\leq 100(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{-l},\;l\in\{0,1,2\}.

Also let
f(x,y):=\mathfrak{T}_{{S}}(x,y)
as in (
H.5
) and
\varphi(r):=\psi(r)r^{2\gamma_{1}-2}
. Note that even though
\varphi
diverges as
r\to 0
, it does so precisely with the decay bounds on
f^{2}
as
r=|x|\to 0
shown in Proposition
H.3
. By an elementary truncation argument, Lemma
H.5
’s (
H.10
) is still applicable with these
\zeta,\varphi,f
. By combining with Proposition
H.3
we get

\displaystyle C(\mathcal{C}_{\circ})^{-1}\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)r^{-2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\cdot(n_{\circ}+2\gamma_{1}-2+|\nu_{y}|^{2})\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)r^{2\gamma_{1}-2}\mathfrak{T}_{{S}}(x,y)^{2}\cdot(n_{\circ}+2\gamma_{1}-2+|\nu_{y}|^{2})\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{\circ})\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\Big[\Big(|\nabla_{\bar{g}}\zeta(y)|\psi(r)+\zeta(y)|\psi^{\prime}(r)|\Big)r^{-1}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\zeta(y)\psi(r)\left(r^{-1}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}\right)r^{\gamma_{1}}|\nabla_{\Sigma,\bar{g}}\mathfrak{T}_{{S}}|\ \Big]d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C(\mathcal{C})\cdot\delta\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}(1+|\nabla\zeta|+|\psi^{\prime}|)\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{\circ}
is strictly stable, we have
n_{\circ}+2\gamma_{1}-2>0
. Using AM-GM on the second term in the final expression, we thus obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)r^{-2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{\circ})\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\Big[\Big(|\nabla\zeta(y)|\psi(r)+\zeta(y)|\psi^{\prime}(r)|\Big)r^{-1}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\zeta(y)\psi(r)r^{2\gamma_{1}}|\nabla_{\Sigma}\mathfrak{T}_{{S}}|^{2}\Big]\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad+C(\mathcal{C})\cdot\delta\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}(1+|\nabla\zeta|+|\psi^{\prime}|)\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}.

Using (
H.9
) and the definition of
\zeta,\psi
we have

(H.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)r^{-2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{C(\mathcal{C}_{\circ})}{\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}}\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-1}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+C(\mathcal{C}_{\circ})\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)(1-\langle\nu_{\Sigma,\bar{g}},\nu_{{S}}\rangle_{\bar{g}})\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}}\cdot\delta\end{split}

It remains to estimate the second to last term of (
H.24
). We may rewrite it as

(H.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)(1-\langle\nu_{\Sigma},\nu_{{S}}\rangle_{\bar{g}})\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad=\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}-\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\langle\nu_{\Sigma,\bar{g}},\nu_{{S}}\rangle_{\bar{g}}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad=\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}-\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\langle\nu_{\Sigma,\bar{g}},\nu_{{S}}\rangle_{\bar{g}}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\ (d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}-d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}})\\
&\qquad=\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\underbrace{\langle\nu_{\mathcal{C},\bar{g}},\nu_{{S}}\rangle_{\bar{g}}}_{=1}\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}-\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\langle\nu_{\Sigma,\bar{g}},\nu_{{S}}\rangle_{\bar{g}}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\ (d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}-d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}).\end{split}

We may estimate the second to last line in (
H.25
) using Stokes’s theorem. To that end, note first that, by (
H.6
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1},\bar{g}}\left(\zeta(y)\psi(r)\nu_{{S}}(x,y)\right)

\displaystyle=\zeta(y)\psi^{\prime}(r)\langle\partial_{r},\nu_{{S}}(x,y)\rangle_{\bar{g}}

and that this is supported in
Q_{\tau_{3}}\cap\{r\geq\tau_{1}\}
. Recall our graph map
\Phi
from (
H.14
) and how, by (
H.15
), there exists a map
u
such that

\Sigma\cap(Q_{\tau_{2}}\setminus\{r<1/4\})=\operatorname{graph}_{\mathcal{C}}u\text{ with }\|u\|_{C^{2}_{*}}<\kappa_{\mathcal{C}}.

Let’s denote
u^{\pm}:=\max\{0,\pm u\}
. Then:

(H.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\langle\nu_{\mathcal{C},\bar{g}},\nu_{{S}}\rangle_{\bar{g}}\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}-\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}\zeta(y)\psi(r)\langle\nu_{\Sigma,\bar{g}},\nu_{{S}}\rangle_{\bar{g}}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\leq\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}\cap\Dom(u)}\int_{-u^{-}}^{u^{+}}\zeta(y)\big|\psi^{\prime}(\sqrt{r^{2}+t^{2}})\langle\partial_{r},\nu_{{S}}\rangle_{\bar{g}}|_{\Phi}\big|\ |\det(D\Phi)|\ dt\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\leq C(\mathcal{C})\cdot\|\psi\|_{C^{1}}\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}\cap\Dom(u)}\int_{-u^{-}}^{u^{+}}r^{-1}|t|\ dt\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}}\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{3}}\cap\Dom(u)}r^{-1}u^{2}\ d\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\bar{g}}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{3}}\cdot\int_{Q_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-1}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}+\frac{C(\mathcal{C})\cdot\delta^{2}}{\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}}\,.\end{split}

In the second inequality we used (
H.13
). In the last inequality, we used Lemma
H.6
.

The result follows by combining (
H.24
), (
H.25
), (
H.26
), and Corollary
H.7
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
H.1
.

Assume
\delta\leq\delta_{1}
in (
H.1
). For each
\tau\in[1/2,1]
, denote for simplicity,

\displaystyle I(\tau):=\int_{Q_{\tau}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\ +\delta\,,

\displaystyle J(\tau):=\int_{Q_{\tau}}r^{-2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\ d\|\Sigma\|_{\bar{g}}\ +\delta\,.

Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
H.8
guarantee, for every
1/2\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq 1
,

(H.27)

J(\tau_{1})\leq\frac{C(\mathcal{C})}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{5}}\sqrt{J(\tau_{2})I(1)}\,.

Let
t_{q}:=1-2^{-q}
,
q\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
.
Taking
\tau_{1}=t_{q},\tau_{2}=t_{q+1}
in (
H.27
), we obtain

\log J(t_{q})\leq C(\mathcal{C})(1+q)+2^{-1}\log J(t_{q+1})+2^{-1}\log I(1)\,.

Multiplying both side by
2^{-q}
and summing with respect to
q\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
we obtain

\log J(1/2)=\log J(t_{1})\leq C(\mathcal{C})+\log I(1)\,.

Combining with Lemma
H.6
, this proves Theorem
H.1
.
∎
